Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile a comprehensive inventory:

## SCINTILLATION/ DEVELOPMENT-STATE INVENTORY

### Directory Structure & Module Map

```
scintillation/
├── scint_analysis/          ← Core pipeline & analysis (MAIN)
├── ne2025/                  ← NE2001 galactic model interface (STANDALONE)
├── notebooks/               ← Jupyter interface (ACTIVE)
├── configs/                 ← YAML configurations
├── chime_acfs/              ← Legacy CHIME data (orphaned)
└── tests/                   ← Test suite
```

---

## CORE MODULE INVENTORY

### **scint_analysis/core.py** (435 lines)
**Domain Role:** ACF computation & dynamic spectrum manipulation  
**Dev State:** `mature` — complete, well-tested  
**Key Functions:**
- `DynamicSpectrum` class: 2D spectrum wrapper (freq×time), downsampling, envelope detection, RFI masking
- `ACF` class: Auto-correlation function container with error handling
- `sigma_clip()`: NumPy-native outlier rejection (avoids astropy bottleneck)

**Entry Points:** Imported by pipeline, widgets, analysis  
**Tests:** `tests/test_core.py` (369 lines, comprehensive)  
**Gaps:** None identified. Pure numpy fallback for astropy.stats dependency already in place.

---

### **scint_analysis/analysis.py** (1,717 lines)
**Domain Role:** ACF fitting, model selection, scintillation parameter derivation, two-screen geometry, scattering↔scintillation consistency  
**Dev State:** `mature` — comprehensive, well-documented  
**Key Functions:**
- **ACF Calculation:**
  - `calculate_acf()`: Lag-domain ACF with finite-scintle error correction
  - `calculate_acf_noerrs()`: Fast version without error propagation
  - Numba JIT fallback for NumPy 2.x incompatibility

- **ACF Model Fitting:**
  - 4 baseline models: Lorentzian, Gaussian, Generalized Lorentzian (power-law), pure power-law
  - `_fit_acf_models()`: Multi-model simultaneous fit with BIC selection
  - `analyze_scintillation_from_acfs()`: Main orchestrator

- **Physics Interpretation (NEW):**
  - `interpret_modulation_index()`: m→emission resolution (Nimmo et al. 2025 framework)
  - `estimate_emission_region_size()`: θ_eff from m and scattering parameters
  - `two_screen_coherence_constraint()`: Mutual coherence limits (Nimmo Eq. 7-11)
  - `scattering_scintillation_consistency()`: τ_d ↔ Δν_DC relation (Bhat et al. 2004)

- **Intra-pulse Evolution:**
  - `analyze_intra_pulse_scintillation()`: Time-sliced ACF tracking

**Entry Points:** Called by `pipeline.ScintillationAnalysis.run()`  
**Tests:** `tests/test_analysis.py` (350 lines, covers models and fitting)  
**Gaps:** 
- Two-screen functions are **present but NOT integrated** into main pipeline (informational only, not called)
- `estimate_emission_region_size()` exists but rarely used
- MCMC posterior sampling mentioned in docstrings but NOT implemented

---

### **scint_analysis/fitting_2d.py** (766 lines)
**Domain Role:** Global frequency-scaling fit across sub-bands (γ(ν) = γ₀×(ν/ν_ref)^α)  
**Dev State:** `mature` — full-featured, wired into pipeline  
**Key Features:**
- `Scintillation2DModel` class: Enforces physical frequency scaling with covariance
- Support for Lorentzian, Generalized Lorentzian, Gaussian, power-law ACF models
- Direct measurement of α (frequency scaling exponent) with proper error propagation
- `gamma_at_freq()`: Evaluate γ at arbitrary frequency with uncertainty

**Entry Points:** Called from `pipeline.ScintillationAnalysis._run_2d_scintillation_fit()`  
**Tests:** None (fitting_2d has no dedicated test file)  
**Gaps:**
- **No unit tests** — only integration through pipeline
- MCMC sampling mentioned in docstring but not implemented
- Physical validation of α bounds (thin screen α≈4.0 vs. Kolmogorov α≈4.4) not enforced

---

### **scint_analysis/pipeline.py** (324 lines)
**Domain Role:** End-to-end orchestration controller  
**Dev State:** `mature` — stable, production-ready  
**Workflow:**
1. Data loading & RFI masking
2. Noise characterization (3 modes: intensity, flux-Gaussian, flux-ShiftedGamma)
3. ACF calculation across sub-bands
4. Model fitting & selection
5. 2D global fit (α estimation)
6. Intra-pulse analysis (optional)
7. Results serialization → JSON

**Entry Points:**
- CLI: `flits-scint` (defined in pyproject.toml)
- Notebook: `widgets.interactive_window_selector()`, `widgets.acf_fitter_dashboard()`

**Cache System:** Pickle caching at each stage (data, ACF, results)  
**Gaps:** None — fully integrated.

---

### **scint_analysis/noise.py** (229 lines)
**Domain Role:** Noise characterization and synthetic generation  
**Dev State:** `mature` — auto-detection of 3 noise flavors  
**Key Classes:**
- `NoiseDescriptor`: Holds parameters for intensity/flux-Gaussian/flux-ShiftedGamma modes
- `estimate_noise_descriptor()`: Auto-detect noise kind + parameters

**Tests:** `tests/test_noise.py` (298 lines, comprehensive)  
**Gaps:** None identified.

---

### **scint_analysis/plotting.py** (1,453 lines)
**Domain Role:** Publication-quality visualization  
**Dev State:** `mature` — extensive plotting library  
**Key Functions:**
- Dynamic spectrum display
- ACF fitting diagnostics
- 2D ACF grids
- Scattering↔scintillation consistency plots
- Power-law scaling visualization

**Gaps:** None critical. Well-developed.

---

### **scint_analysis/physics.py** (243 lines)
**Domain Role:** Physical scaling relations for derived parameters  
**Dev State:** `mature` — utility functions  
**Functions:**
- `scintillation_bandwidth_to_timescale()`: Δν_d → τ_d via uncertainty relation
- `effective_velocity()`: Transverse velocity from scintillation + geometry
- `screen_distance_from_curvature()`: D_eff from arc curvature
- `weighted_avg_and_std()`: Robust statistics helper

**Gaps:** None. Stable utilities.

---

### **scint_analysis/config.py** (277 lines)
**Domain Role:** YAML config loading & path resolution  
**Dev State:** `mature` — robust, production-ready  
**Features:**
- Path resolution (env vars, home dir expansion, relative paths)
- Workspace root auto-detection
- Telescope + burst config merging

**Gaps:** None.

---

### **scint_analysis/consistency.py** (119 lines)
**Domain Role:** Post-analysis consistency check between scattering & scintillation  
**Dev State:** `partial` — functional but minimal  
**Function:** `run_consistency_check()` — loads both pipelines' JSON outputs, generates cross-check plot  
**Gaps:** 
- Standalone utility, NOT wired into main pipeline
- Data extraction logic assumes specific JSON schema (brittle)

---

### **scint_analysis/widgets.py** (785 lines)
**Domain Role:** Interactive Jupyter notebook interface  
**Dev State:** `mature` — optional ipywidgets dependency  
**Key Widgets:**
- `interactive_window_selector()`: Select on/off-pulse windows via slider
- `acf_fitter_dashboard()`: Manual ACF model fitting with live plotting

**Gaps:** Requires `ipywidgets` (gracefully disabled if unavailable).

---

### **scint_analysis/run_analysis.py** (96 lines)
**Domain Role:** CLI entry point  
**Dev State:** `mature` — minimal, delegates to pipeline  
**Functionality:**
- Argument parsing (burst config YAML)
- Pipeline execution
- JSON result serialization with NumpyJSONEncoder

**Entry Point:** `flits-scint` console script  
**Gaps:** None.

---

## NE2001 INTEGRATION

### **ne2025/query_ne2025_scint.py** (103 lines)
**Domain Role:** Galactic scattering floor prediction from sky coordinates  
**Dev State:** `stub/experimental` — **NOT integrated into main pipeline**  
**Features:**
- Query `mwprop.nemod.NE2025` for τ_scatt & Δν_scint predictions
- Handles multiple coordinate formats (ICRS, sexagesimal, degrees)
- Batch processing CSV → CSV with MW floor predictions

**Status:**
- Standalone CLI tool (not imported by scint_analysis)
- Can be run separately to generate reference scattering floors
- Two-screen / scattering↔scintillation cross-analysis **NOT present** in main pipeline

**Gaps:**
- **Not wired** into pipeline or results comparison
- No validation against measured values
- Manual post-hoc use only

---

## DEVELOPMENT STATE CLASSIFICATION

| Module | Classification | Justification |
|--------|-----------------|---------------|
| core.py | **mature** | Complete, tested, no outstanding issues |
| analysis.py | **mature** | Comprehensive, well-documented, tested |
| fitting_2d.py | **mature** | Full-featured, wired into pipeline, but NO unit tests |
| pipeline.py | **mature** | Production-ready, stable orchestration |
| noise.py | **mature** | Complete characterization, tested |
| plotting.py | **mature** | Extensive, no known gaps |
| physics.py | **mature** | Stable utility functions |
| config.py | **mature** | Robust, production-ready |
| widgets.py | **mature** | Optional, well-designed |
| consistency.py | **partial** | Functional but loosely integrated, brittle JSON parsing |
| ne2025/query_ne2025_scint.py | **stub/experimental** | Standalone CLI, NOT integrated into pipeline |
| run_analysis.py | **mature** | Minimal entry point |

---

## CRITICAL FINDINGS

### ✅ STRENGTHS
1. **Comprehensive ACF fitting**: 4 models + BIC selection + proper error propagation
2. **Two-screen geometry logic**: Present and correct (Nimmo et al. 2025 framework)
3. **Scattering↔scintillation consistency**: Implemented with proper physics
4. **2D global fitting**: Enforces physical frequency scaling (γ ∝ ν^α) correctly
5. **Noise characterization**: 3 distribution types with auto-detection
6. **Test coverage**: ~1K lines for core modules (core, analysis, noise)
7. **Publication-ready plotting**: Extensive visualization library
8. **Clean architecture**: Pipeline orchestrator, modular design, YAML config

### ⚠️ GAPS & LIMITATIONS
1. **NE2001/galactic integration**: Query tool exists but is **completely standalone**, not wired into pipeline
   - No validation of measured scintillation against predicted MW floor
   - Manual post-hoc use only
   
2. **Two-screen cross-analysis**: Functions exist but are **informational only**, not called during analysis
   - `two_screen_coherence_constraint()` not invoked by pipeline
   - `scattering_scintillation_consistency()` not integrated with scattering pipeline results
   - Requires manual post-hoc cross-reference with separate scattering output
   
3. **Missing unit tests for fitting_2d.py**: No dedicated test suite for 2D model fitting (most critical function!)
   
4. **Consistency module**: Loose integration, brittle JSON schema assumptions
   
5. **MCMC sampling**: Mentioned in docstrings but not implemented
   
6. **Intra-pulse analysis**: Functional but rarely used, minimal documentation on interpreting evolution
   
7. **Legacy code**: `chime_acfs/` directory present but orphaned

### 🎯 ENTRY POINTS
- **CLI**: `flits-scint /path/to/burst_dsa.yaml`
- **Notebook**: `scintillation_analysis.ipynb` (interactive widgets)
- **Library**: `from scint_analysis import pipeline; pipe = pipeline.ScintillationAnalysis(cfg); pipe.run()`
- **Post-hoc tool**: `query_ne2025_scint.py` (standalone galactic model check)
- **Consistency check**: `consistency.py` (manual cross-pipeline validation)

---

## RECOMMENDED NEXT STEPS

1. **Add unit tests for fitting_2d.py** (critical path)
2. **Wire NE2001 validation** into pipeline (optional post-processing step)
3. **Integrate two-screen checks** as optional diagnostic output
4. **Refactor consistency.py** to accept parsed ACF objects instead of JSON
5. **Document intra-pulse evolution** interpretation framework
6. **Clean up chime_acfs/** (archive or remove)

## SIMULATION SUBSYSTEM INVENTORY

### 1. MODULE MAP + DEVELOPMENT STATE

|----------|-----------------|---------------|-----------------|
| **simulation/engine.py** (825 LOC) | Forward simulator engine: coherent two-screen multipath propagation, delay calculation, IRF convolution with intrinsic pulse | **Mature** | Complete, well-tested; 28 methods; Numba JIT acceleration integrated (optional); handles geometric delays, Doppler velocity effects, dynamic spectra synthesis; two code paths for delays (fast/slow Taylor vs. exact recompute) validated in tests |
| **simulation/wave_optics.py** (447 LOC) | Wave-optics alternative: Fresnel propagation via angular spectrum method; Kolmogorov phase screen generation | **Partial/Spike** | Core structure present but incomplete; phase screen generation works; propagation implemented; however, spatial-domain interpretation ambiguous (point-source vs. plane-wave handling is documented as provisional); no validation campaign yet; serves as research prototype for diffraction-dominated regime |
| **simulation/screen.py** (83 LOC) | Scattering screen realisation: random phase and amplitude generation (Gaussian/Rayleigh distribution, anisotropic geometry support) | **Mature** | Complete; supports 1D/2D screens, anisotropic elliptical profiles, position angle rotation, constant/Rayleigh amplitude distributions |
| **simulation/geometry.py** (27 LOC) | Cosmological distance utilities (angular-diameter distance, 2-vector normalisation) | **Mature** | Thin utility layer; complete |
| **simulation/instrument.py** (30 LOC) | Instrumental noise model (SEFD, T_sys/A_eff, thermal noise generator) | **Mature** | Complete; physically-grounded noise variance derivation |
| **simulation/sim_fit_bridge.py** (119 LOC) | Sim ↔ Fitter adapter: unit conversion, simulator output → burstfit MCMC input | **Mature** | Complete round-trip wiring; injection-recovery tested; tau recovery (smoke test, not quantitative) |
| **simulation/analysis_utils.py** (91 LOC) | Theoretical observables calculator + distance estimators (Main 2022, Ocker 2022, Sammons 2023, Pradeep 2025) | **Mature** | Complete; validates against formula collection |

| **simulation/recreate_figures.py** (411 LOC) | Figure reproduction suite (Pradeep et al. 2025 Figs 1, 4, 6, 7, 8, 15) | **Partial** | Scaffolding and configs present; actual figure generation methods incomplete (Fig 6 method references undefined `simulate_scattered_time_series`, line 130) |
| **simulation/validate_unresolved_case.py** (103 LOC) | Validation: unresolved two-screen regime; ACF peak should equal m² = 3 (Eq. 4.26) | **Mature** | Working validation script; produces plots |
| **simulation/validate_doppler_rate.py** (128 LOC) | Validation: Doppler-rate time evolution vs. theory (Appendix A, Eq. A.4–A.5) | **Mature** | Complete validation method; secondary spectrum analysis |
| **simulation/validate_wave_optics.py** (113 LOC) | Validation: wave-optics modulation index (unresolved regime target: m ≈ √3) | **Stub/Experimental** | Scaffold with comments on feasibility gaps; acknowledges Gpc-scale simulations impossible with realistic grids; "Toy model" approach placeholder |
| **simulation/analyze_burst.py** (154 LOC) | Burst profile analysis utilities | **Orphaned/Legacy** | Not invoked by tests; function names suggest legacy interface |
| **simulation/frb_scintillator.py** (16 LOC) | Compatibility façade (re-exports for backward compatibility) | **Mature** | Clean module aggregator |

- **recreate_figures.py**: Configs present; figure methods unfinished
- **monte_carlo.py**: Trial loop complete; results aggregation incomplete
- **multifreq_analysis.py**: Broadband loop structure present; analysis endpoints missing

### 3. ENTRY POINTS & CONSUMPTION

#### **How Simulator Is Invoked**

```python
# Primary invocation pattern (engine.py)
sim = FRBScintillator(cfg: SimCfg)
I_t_nu = sim.synthesise_dynamic_spectrum(duration, ...)  # time-freq dynamic spectrum
# or
I_freq = sim.simulate_time_integrated_spectrum()        # frequency-only spectrum
# or
I_t = sim.simulate_1d_time_series(duration, ...)         # time-only series
```

#### **Who Consumes the Output**

1. **Direct analysis** → {validate_unresolved_case, validate_doppler_rate, sim_fit_validation.py}
2. **Fitter validation** → sim_fit_bridge → FRBFitter (scattering/scat_analysis/burstfit.py)
3. **Ensemble studies** → monte_carlo.py, multifreq_analysis.py (incomplete pipelines)

#### **Integration Health**

- ✓ **Simulation → Fit bridge**: Fully wired and tested (test_sim_fit_roundtrip.py)
- ✓ **Simulator internals**: Physics tests present (test_simulation_doppler.py)
- ✗ **Parameter-recovery ensemble**: Incomplete scaffolding
- ✗ **Wave-optics validation**: No quantitative test (only feasibility notes)

### 4. GAPS & TODOs

#### **Code-Level Issues**
- **No TODO/FIXME/XXX markers** found in simulation/*.py — discipline is good but gaps are implicit

#### **Structural Gaps**

| **Gap** | **Impact** | **Evidence** |
|---------|-----------|------------|
| **Wave-optics incomplete** | Spike code; no real validation campaign | Comments in validate_wave_optics.py (lines 60–73) explain Gpc simulation infeasibility; phase screen + propagation present but spatial-domain interpretation ambiguous (point vs. plane wave at source) |
| **Figure reproduction unfinished** | paper-matching plots incomplete | recreate_figures.py: Fig 6 method references undefined `simulate_scattered_time_series` method (not in engine.py); line 130 will fail |
| **Monte Carlo aggregation stub** | Results unreliable | monte_carlo.py line 119 (DataFrame construction) orphaned; trial loop completes but downstream aggregation missing |
| **Multifreq analysis incomplete** | Broadband RP evolution plot (Fig 16) cannot be generated | multifreq_analysis.py: ACF fitting loop starts ~line 99–100 but method body is cut short; no results collection |
| **No quantitative tau-recovery validation** | Fitter working but recovery accuracy unknown | test_sim_fit_roundtrip.py explicitly disclaims quantitative validation (docstring line 9–11); notes simulator IRF model ≠ exponential PBF fitter model; only smoke test present |

#### **Half-Built Functions**

- `recreate_figures.py::generate_figure_6()` (line 120): calls undefined `sim.simulate_scattered_time_series()` → should be `sim.simulate_1d_time_series()`
- `multifreq_analysis.py::run_broadband_analysis()` (line ~100): loop structure present, inner trial aggregation missing
- `monte_carlo.py::run_monte_carlo_parallel()` (line 97+): ProcessPoolExecutor loop works, but final DataFrame aggregation at line 119 is orphaned

#### **Known Physics Limitations (Documented)**

- **Wave-optics Gpc scaling impossible**: validate_wave_optics.py (lines 60–80) notes `dx < r0` constraint → 10^20 grid pixels required for Gpc + realistic r0 → infeasible
- **Single-realisation tau recovery loose**: sim_fit_bridge.py roundtrip uses M2 (exponential PBF), simulator produces coherent multipath IRF; ensemble averaging needed for clean recovery (documented in scripts/sim_fit_validation.py docstring lines 14–21)
- **Scintillation suppresses profile**: validate_unresolved_case.py line 86 filters out near-noise time bins; 2D engine produce high-variance per-slice spectra

### 5. ENTRY POINTS, CALLERS & ORCHESTRATION

#### **Public API (FRBScintillator methods)**

| **Method** | **Purpose** | **Used By** |
|-----------|-----------|-----------|
| `simulate_time_integrated_spectrum()` | Single-epoch frequency spectrum | validate_unresolved_case, multifreq_analysis (partial), monte_carlo |
| `synthesise_dynamic_spectrum()` | Full 2D I(t, ν) via STFT | sim_fit_validation, test_sim_fit_roundtrip |
| `synthesise_dynamic_spectrum_2d()` | True 2D (exact per-timestep IRF) | (not in use; slower variant) |
| `synthesise_dynamic_spectrum_2d_parallel()` | 2D + parallelized | (documented; not actively used) |
| `simulate_1d_time_series()` | Time-only intensity | (referenced in recreate_figures but undefined in engine) |
| `acf()`, `fit_acf_robust()` | Spectral ACF + two-component fit | validate_unresolved_case, monte_carlo, multifreq_analysis |
| `calculate_theoretical_observables()` | Theory: tau_s, nu_s | validate_unresolved_case, monte_carlo, sim_fit_validation |
| `get_irf_spikes()` | IRF spike decomposition | (recreate_figures Fig 6, incomplete) |
| `analyze_intra_pulse_scintillation()` | Time-slice scintillation evolution | (documented; not used) |

#### **Cross-Module Dependencies**

```
engine.py → screen.py → geometry.py (cosmological distances, anisotropy)
         → instrument.py (noise model)
         
wave_optics.py → geometry.py (same)
              → (no dep on screen.py; generates own Kolmogorov screens)

sim_fit_bridge.py → engine.py (FRBScintillator, SimCfg)
                 → scattering/scat_analysis/burstfit.py (FRBModel, FRBFitter)
                 
sim_fit_validation.py (script) → engine.py + burstfit.py (full roundtrip)
                              → tools/figure_manifest.py (results archiving)
```

### 6. SUMMARY: DEVELOPMENT STATE BY CONFIDENCE LEVEL

| **Confidence** | **Modules** | **Notes** |
|---|---|---|
| **Production-Ready** | engine.py, screen.py, geometry.py, instrument.py, sim_fit_bridge.py | Fully tested; physics validated; used in roundtrip; Doppler effects verified to 1e-18 floating-point tolerance |
| **Research-Grade** | validate_unresolved_case.py, validate_doppler_rate.py, scripts/sim_fit_validation.py | Working validation campaigns; produce figures; documented limitations (scintillation scatter in single realisations) |
| **Incomplete/Experimental** | wave_optics.py, monte_carlo.py, multifreq_analysis.py, recreate_figures.py | Scaffolding present; physics structure sound; endpoints missing or incorrect method references; feasibility limits documented (Gpc infeasibility for wave optics) |
| **Orphaned** | analyze_burst.py, frb_scintillator.py (façade) | Legacy or re-export layer; not invoked directly |

### 7. VALIDATION PHILOSOPHY

The codebase follows a **two-tier validation model**:

1. **Unit/Integration Tests** (`tests/`): Wiring, consistency (fast/slow Doppler paths), shape contracts
2. **Physics Validation Scripts** (`simulation/validate_*.py`, `scripts/sim_fit_validation.py`): Reproduce paper results, match theory (ACF peak = 3, Doppler rates, etc.)
   - **Single-realisation**: High scintillation variance; good for IRF visualization; tau recovery loose
   - **Ensemble averaging** (documented but incomplete in code): Suppresses scintillation; produces smooth exponential PBF tail; quantitative tau recovery clean

The **gap**: ensemble-averaging validation pipeline is scaffolded (monte_carlo.py) but unfinished. Single-realisation tests pass; ensemble-backed quantitative tau recovery is documented as needed but not implemented end-to-end.

---

### CONCLUSION

**Simulator (engine.py) is mature & production-ready**. Wave-optics is an experimental spike. Validation test coverage is good for wiring; physical validation campaigns are complete (unresolved ACF, Doppler rates) but limited to single realizations. Parameter-recovery quantification (ensemble tau fitting) is 70% scaffolded.

## GAPS & KNOWN ISSUES

### **Incomplete / Partial modules:**
1. **priors_physical.py**: Soft dependency on astropy + mwprop. Gracefully degraded if missing (try/except in __init__.py). ~70% implemented; NE2001 calls are mocked in tests.
2. **dm_preprocessing.py**: Wraps external dispersion.dmphasev2; ~60% complete, no dedicated tests. Used only if refine_dm=true in config.
3. **burst_metadata.py**: Minimal CSV loader; ~40% complete. Pandas-dependent.
4. **burstfit_interactive.py**: Depends on ipywidgets (commented out of __init__.py). Jupyter-only feature.

### **Untested modules:**
- **burstfit_robust.py**: Post-fit diagnostics (subband_consistency, jackknife, etc.) used by BurstDiagnostics but no dedicated tests.
- **burstfit_joint.py**: Multi-band fit example; not imported into main pipeline, not tested.
- **burstfit_corner.py**: Plotting utility; used by run_scat_analysis.py but no unit tests.
- **visualization.py**: Diagnostic plots; no unit tests (plotting is hard to test).
- **pipeline/optimization.py**: MLE refinement + burn/thin; tested indirectly but not dedicated.
- **pool_utils.py**: Simple; tested manually via CLI.

### **TODOs/FIXMEs (from grep):**
- **burstfit_joint.py:524**: "precompute log-bounds with safe placeholders (log(1)=0)"—minor comment, not a blocker.
- **pipeline/core.py:1002**: "Greedy BIC-based component selection (placeholder)"—feature earmarked but not implemented (ncomp > 1 path incomplete).
- **pipeline/core.py:1004**: "Earmarks / placeholders"—component selection framework sketched but not active.

### **Over-engineering / duplication:**
- **burstfit_modelselect.py vs burstfit_nested.py**: Two parallel model selection paths (BIC vs evidence). Both work; no overlap, but users must choose. This is intentional (paper trades off robustness vs speed).
- **burstfit_robust.py**: Multiple diagnostic checks (subband, jackknife, dm_opt, subband_profiles) bundled together; could be more modular.
- **Three separate initialization paths**: data_driven_initial_guess(), quick_initial_guess(), manual via InitialGuessWidget—appropriate given use cases (batch, quick, interactive).

### **Abandonment flags:**
- **compare_resolutions.py**: Research spike, incomplete. Not imported elsewhere.
- **simple_runtime_test.py**, **test_quick_runtime.py**: Scaffolding. Not in test suite.
- **notebooks/update_nb_path.py**: Utility for notebook path setup; vestigial.

  │     ├─ subband_consistency()
  │     ├─ leave_one_out_influence()
  │     ├─ dm_optimization_check()
  │     ├─ fit_subband_profiles()

  ├─ save results → JSON + HDF5
  └─ plot corner + summary

### 7. STATED SCIENCE SCOPE (from mkdocs.yml & docs/)

The project targets:

1. **Scattering vs. frequency:** Multi-screen geometry → two-scale scintillation visibility in fine-resolution data (Pradeep et al. 2025 context)
2. **Scintillation-scattering consistency:** Reconcile τ_s and ν_s predictions across a two-screen ISM+host model
3. **DM budget refinement:** Partition observed DM into MW, intergalactic, intervening galaxy, and host components
4. **Host galaxy localization:** Cross-match GLADE, measure sightline impact parameter and CGM mass
5. **Multi-component model selection:** Use BIC to identify how many discrete scattering screens / interference fringes are resolvable in dynamic spectra

## FLITS Codebase Architecture Map + Development State Inventory

---

### **1. DOMAIN ROLE MATRIX**

| **Module** | **Domain Role** | **LOC** | **Dev State** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **flits/batch/** | Multi-burst scattering/scintillation batch runner → SQLite results_db, joint τ-Δν analysis, summary/export (CSV/LaTeX/JSON), config generation | 2670 | **partial** |
| **flits/fitting/** | Fit quality diagnostics + residual validation thresholds (MCMC convergence, chi², R², parameter bounds, physics constraints) | ~200 | **mature** |
| **flits/orchestration/** | Maistro provenance sidecars for galaxy-search + DM/scattering budget attestation to canonical state (trusted tau promotion gate) | 535 | **mature** |
| **flits/scattering/** | Thin namespace bridge → legacy scattering.scat_analysis (burstfit models M0–M3, MCMC, Bayesian evidence, priors); exports broaden utilities | ~30 | **thin wrapper** |
| **galaxies/v2_0/** | Host-galaxy association pipeline: Vizier + NED foreground search, redshift deduplication, PS1/DESI/ALLWISE enrichment, mNFW CGM DM/tau prediction (phi-budget decomposition into MW + cosmic + intervening + host) | 6793 | **mature** |
| **crossmatching/** | Multi-telescope TOA + geometric-delay cross-match for CHIME↔DSA co-detection inference (K_DM delays, baseline baseline vectors) | 709 | **stub/experimental** |
| **dispersion/** | DM-phase coherence estimator via FFT bootstrap; quadratic peak-fitting for DM uncertainty; frequency-reference indifferent | 239 | **partial** |

---

### **2. DEVELOPMENT STATE JUSTIFICATIONS**

#### **Mature (production-grade)**

- **flits/fitting/**: Complete VALIDATION_THRESHOLDS + ResidualDiagnostics (normality, bias, autocorr, chi²/R² gates). Well-structured.
- **flits/orchestration/**: Maistro RPC client fully wired; build_budget_payloads + promotion logic for quality gates (PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL). Tests for sightline budgets exist.
- **galaxies/v2_0/**: 
  - Search: full Vizier + NED + DESI DR1 TAP query chains; foreground/background split by z_frb (with placeholder z=1.0 sentinel); cross-catalog deduplication (by separation + redshift agreement).
  - Enrichment: PS1, DESI-Legacy, ALLWISE, GALEX, 2MASS cross-match with fallback logic.
  - Budget: Macquart cosmic DM, mNFW hot-halo ρ(r), cool CGM two-phase screen; interior-cap for low impact; yield DM + τ budgets with regime verdicts ("intervening_candidate", "host-dominated", etc.).
  - **Tests**: 10 test files, 84+ test cases covering engines, deduplication, enrichment, sightline_budget, sightline_sensitivity (Monte Carlo sample draws).

#### **Partial (feature-complete, design debt)**

- **flits/batch/**: 
  - Orchestrates multi-burst MCMC over SQLite; config_generator, joint_analysis, summary_plots, results export functional.
  - **TODOs**: 
    - Scintillation config generation stubbed (TODO lines in batch_runner.py:88, 197).
    - τ scaling placeholder (analysis_logic.py, line ~110: "A robust implementation would...").
  - **Tests**: 29 cases (test_batch.py).

- **dispersion/dmphasev2.py**:
  - DMPhaseEstimator vectorizes FFT coherence across DM grid, bootstrap resamples, quadratic peak-fitting.
  - Works but incomplete: no handling of frequency-dependent RFI masking (f_cut optional but minimal); no integration with galaxy/MW models.

#### **Stub/Experimental (present but not operational)**

- **crossmatching/**:
  - toa_crossmatch.py: Function stubs compute_toa, compute_geometric_delay (CHIME/DSA baseline geometry, K_DM delays).
  - **Major gap**: Actual cross-match logic (TOA residual χ², co-detection confidence, handle clock offset / CLOCK_ERR_MS) is **not implemented**. The code shows example usage (dm_opt, CHIME/DSA frequency bands) but lacks:
    - Likelihood for joint CHIME+DSA DM estimation.
    - Systematic clock jitter handling.
    - Statistical hypothesis test (H₀: co-detected vs. independent noise).
  - toa_utilities.py is partial (delay utilities, but no joint-inference engine).
  - **Tests**: 73 lines (test.py), minimal coverage.

#### **Thin Wrapper (architectural debt)**

- **flits/scattering/**: 
  - Namespace bridge only (11 lines). Re-exports broaden + priors from canonical scattering.scat_analysis.
  - **Rationale**: Installable package structure (via flits namespace) but logic lives in scattering/ (legacy, ~7500 LOC).
  - Not duplication per se, but adds indirection. Single source of truth: scattering.scat_analysis.burstfit (models, MCMC, nested sampling).

---

### **3. ENTRY POINTS & CALLERS**

| **CLI / Script** | **Role** | **Callers** |
|---|---|---|
| `flits-batch run` | Dispatch multi-burst scattering/scintillation batch | BatchRunner → _run_scattering_analysis (isolated worker) + _run_scintillation_analysis; populate SQLite results_db |
| `flits-batch generate-configs` | Scan data dirs, emit burst configs | ConfigGenerator → BurstInfo manifests |
| `flits-batch joint-analysis` | Load results_db → check τ-Δν consistency, frequency scaling, plots | JointAnalysis (analysis_logic + joint_analysis_plots) |
| `flits-batch summary` | Generate summary plots (τ vs freq, Δν vs freq, 2D scatter) | SummaryPlots engine |
| `flits-batch export` | Dump results to CSV/LaTeX/JSON | ResultsDatabase → Pandas export |
| `flits-scat` | Legacy scattering single-burst entry | scattering.run_scat_analysis (not refactored under flits/) |
| `flits-scint` | Legacy scintillation single-burst entry | scintillation.scint_analysis.run_analysis (parallel code path) |

**Call Graph**:
```
flits-batch run
  ├─ BatchConfig + ConfigGenerator
  ├─ BatchRunner.run_all()
  │  ├─ _run_scattering_analysis (multiprocess worker)
  │  │  └─ flits.scattering.scat_analysis.pipeline.BurstPipeline
  │  │     └─ burstfit (M0–M3 model comparison, MCMC)
  │  └─ _run_scintillation_analysis (multiprocess worker)
  │     └─ scintillation.scint_analysis.pipeline
  │        └─ 2D ACF fitting
  └─ ResultsDatabase (SQLite persist)

flits-batch joint-analysis results_db.sqlite
  └─ JointAnalysis
     ├─ check_tau_deltanu_consistency (analysis_logic)
     ├─ analyze_frequency_scaling (analysis_logic)
     └─ generate_summary_plots (joint_analysis_plots)

flits-orchestration/maistro.py
  └─ galaxy-search provenance (Maistro RPC)

flits-orchestration/maistro_scattering.py
  └─ DM/scattering budget provenance (Maistro RPC)
     └─ galaxies.v2_0.sightline_budget (pulls foreground predictions)
```

---

### **4. GAPS & HALF-BUILT FUNCTIONALITY**

| **Gap** | **Severity** | **Location** | **Notes** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Co-detection cross-match** | **CRITICAL** | crossmatching/ | Geometry + delay functions present but no joint likelihood, clock-offset model, or co-detection χ². Aspirational only. |
| **Scintillation config generation** | **HIGH** | flits/batch/batch_runner.py:88, 197 | TODO comments; scintillation results stored but configs not auto-generated. |
| **τ frequency scaling** | **MEDIUM** | flits/batch/analysis_logic.py:110 | Placeholder comment: "A robust implementation would..." Likely needs detailed frequency-dependent τ(ν) model. |
| **Host-galaxy DM/τ prediction** | **MEDIUM** | galaxies/v2_0/ | Budget decomposes into MW + cosmic + intervening + **host (residual)**. Host term is derived, not modeled—no halo model for unknown-z hosts. |
| **RFI masking in DM estimation** | **LOW** | dispersion/dmphasev2.py:92 | f_cut (freq window) optional but minimal; no per-frequency RFI flag handling. |
| **Placeholder redshift handling** | **LOW** | galaxies/v2_0/config.py:77, sightline_budget.py | z_frb=1.0 sentinel for unmeasured (Freya/Mahi/Johndoeii); cosmic DM + foreground/background split withheld. Documented, intentional. |

### **6. ARCHITECTURAL DEBT & OVER-ENGINEERING**

1. **Dual Entry Points (scattering + flits.scattering)**:
   - `flits-scat` points to `scattering.run_scat_analysis:main` (legacy location).
   - `flits.batch.cli` imports from `flits.scattering.scat_analysis.pipeline` (wrapped namespace).
   - Single source of truth (scattering/) is solid, but namespace indirection creates confusion in imports.
   - **Recommendation**: Consolidate to single import style or deprecate scattering/ top-level CLI.

2. **Parallel Scintillation Path**:
   - scintillation/ is independent (~6500 LOC), not integrated into flits.batch until recent refactoring.
   - Config generation for scintillation remains stubbed (TODO lines 88, 197 in batch_runner.py).
   - Opportunity to merge config generation + validation thresholds.

3. **Placeholder Redshift Sentinel**:
   - z_frb=1.0 hard-coded in galaxies/v2_0/config.py:TARGETS and sightline_budget.py:PLACEHOLDER_Z.
   - Intentional (flags cosmic/host DM terms as withheld), well-tested, but rigid.
   - Consider parametrized config for multi-survey reuse.

---

### **7. ENTRY POINTS TO REAL PHYSICS**

| **Physics Question** | **Module(s)** | **Completeness** |
|---|---|---|
| **What is τ_obs for a burst?** | flits.scattering → scattering.scat_analysis.burstfit (M0–M3 model fit via MCMC) | ✓ Mature |
| **What is Δν_obs for a burst?** | scintillation.scint_analysis → 2D ACF fitting | ✓ Mature |
| **Is τ × Δν physically consistent?** | flits.batch.analysis_logic.check_tau_deltanu_consistency | ✓ Functional |
| **What is the frequency scaling α of τ and Δν?** | flits.batch.analysis_logic.analyze_frequency_scaling | ⚠ Placeholder logic |
| **Which galaxy(ies) foreground the sightline?** | galaxies.v2_0.search (Vizier + NED query, dedup, enrich) | ✓ Mature |
| **What DM/τ does a foreground contribute?** | galaxies.v2_0.scattering_predict (mNFW + two-phase screen) | ✓ Mature |
| **How much of DM_obs comes from MW/cosmic/intervening/host?** | galaxies.v2_0.sightline_budget | ✓ Mature (host is residual) |
| **Are two bursts co-detected (same source)?** | crossmatching.toa_crossmatch | ✗ **NOT IMPLEMENTED** |
| **What is the DM phase coherence from FFT?** | dispersion.dmphasev2.DMPhaseEstimator | ✓ Functional (minimal RFI handling) |

## **CONCLUSIONS**

### **Strengths**
1. **Core physics robust**: scattering (M0–M3, MCMC), scintillation (ACF), and galaxy budgets (mNFW CGM, foreground search) are well-engineered.
2. **Batch orchestration solid**: multi-burst runner, results DB, joint analysis, export pipelines functional and tested.
3. **Provenance tied to Maistro**: canonical state (galaxy search + DM/tau budgets) makes sightlines auditable.
4. **Galaxies module comprehensive**: search + enrichment + CGM physics tightly coupled; budget decomposition (MW+cosmic+intervening+host) complete.

### **Critical Gaps**
1. **Co-detection logic missing**: crossmatching/ has function stubs but **no actual CHIME↔DSA joint inference engine**. Clock offset, likelihood, hypothesis test all absent. This is currently **aspirational**.
2. **Scintillation integration incomplete**: config generation stubbed; scintillation runs in isolation.
3. **τ scaling placeholder**: frequency-dependence logic marked "TODO"; needs physics-based model.

### **Technical Debt**
1. Dual scattering entry points (scattering/ + flits.scattering namespace) → import confusion.
2. Placeholder z=1.0 sentinel hard-coded (works but not parameterized for reuse).
3. Under-tested modules: crossmatching (5 test cases), dispersion (10 test cases).

### **Modularity Assessment**
- **flits/**: Thin orchestration wrapper over scattering + scintillation (not duplication, by design).
- **galaxies/v2_0/**: Self-contained, can be used independently for sightline budget.
- **crossmatching/**: Currently isolated; requires co-detection engine to become operational.
- **dispersion/**: Functional but not integrated into batch pipeline; phase estimation is standalone.

---

**Summary**: The codebase is **production-ready for scattering + scintillation fitting + galaxy association**, with **mature** test coverage for those domains. **Cross-matching (co-detection) is an unimplemented skeleton** and should not be treated as operational. Scintillation integration and τ-scaling physics are **partially complete** and marked with TODOs.

## Summary

I have extracted a comprehensive **quantitative per-burst capability matrix** from the result files for your 12 CHIME/DSA co-detected FRBs. Here are the critical findings:

### **KEY DATA SOURCES EXTRACTED:**

1. **Scattering joint fits** (11 of 12 bursts): `analysis/scattering-refit-2026-06/joint_json/*.json`
   - Casey: Missing from joint_json (only DSA-band adv_casey_results.json exists)
   - All others: tau_1ghz & alpha with posteriors

2. **Scintillation ACF measurements** (3 of 12 bursts): `*_multiscale_results.json` + `adv_casey_results.json`
   - **Casey**: dν_CHIME=2.16 MHz (m²=0.0997), dν_DSA=5.51 MHz (m²=0.4981) 
   - **Freya**: dν_CHIME=12.93 MHz (m²=0.2066), dν_DSA=6.99 MHz (m²=0.0513) — **INVERSE SCALING (ν⁴ violation)**
   - **Wilhelm**: dν_CHIME=9.95 MHz (m²=0.1957), dν_DSA=2.72 MHz (m²=0.0470) — **INVERSE SCALING (unphysical)**
   - **9 others**: No ACF fits generated

3. **Quality flags & DM budgets**: `sightline_dm_scattering_budget.csv`
   - Casey, Freya, Wilhelm: **FAIL** (chi2_red = 69.2, 3.9, 1.4 respectively); tau withheld
   - DM components per burst provided (MW ISM, halo, cosmic, intervening, host)

4. **Host associations**: `search_summary.csv`

---

### **CRITICAL FINDINGS - BINDING LIMITATIONS FOR SCATTERING+SCINTILLATION LOCALIZATION:**

| Burst | τ@1GHz (ms) | α | Scintillation Status | **BINDING LIMITATION** |
|-------|---|---|---|---|
| **Casey** | ABSENT (FAIL) | ABSENT | CHIME+DSA measured but τ_obs FAIL χ²=69.2 | High scattering, CHIME ACF only, tau withheld |
| **Chromatica** | 0.0249±0.0002 | 5.999 | NO ACF fits | Precision-limited (shot-noise floor); α unconstrained |
| **Freya** | 0.0487±0.0036 (refit) vs 3.515 (legacy) | 5.995 | **INVERSE**: Δν_DSA/CHIME=0.658 (expect 30.03) ⟹ α_scint=−0.49 | **52× LEGACY DISCREPANCY, FAIL χ²=3.9, inverse ν (refractive), placeholder z** |
| **Hamilton** | 0.00497±0.00006 | 5.990 | NO ACF fits | Extremely weak τ (5 μs); tau-limited; insufficient dynamic range |
| **Isha** | 0.347 +0.239 −0.220 | 4.961 +0.849 −2.598 | NO ACF fits | **Highly degenerate α** (range 2.36–5.81); ±70% error bar; weak-scatt ambiguous |
| **JohnDoeII** | 0.852±0.020 | 1.373 | NO ACF fits | **Placeholder z**, large intervening DM (390 pc/cm³); budget unclosed |
| **Mahi** | 0.0945 +0.031 −0.018 | 5.530 +0.322 −0.419 | NO ACF fits | **Placeholder z**, zero intervening DM (anomalous); broad α; high MW ISM |
| **Oran** | 0.497 +0.111 −0.060 | 1.439 +0.661 −0.325 | NO ACF fits | **Extremely broad α** (1.11–2.10); ±46% error; regime ambiguous; zero intervening |
| **Phineas** | 0.322±0.005 | 3.578 | NO ACF fits | **DM over-constrained**: intervening 712 >> residual; negative host DM (−415); τ_int dominates |
| **Whitney** | 0.486±0.011 | 1.458 | NO ACF fits | **DM over-constrained**: intervening 364 >> residual; negative host; weak-α anomalous |
| **Wilhelm** | 0.2607±0.0043 (refit) vs 2.818 (legacy) | 2.706 | **INVERSE**: Δν_DSA/CHIME=0.254 ⟹ α_scint=−1.61 (IMPOSSIBLE) | **10× LEGACY DISCREPANCY, FAIL χ²=1.4, inverse ν (refractive), negative host** |
| **Zach** | 0.3219±0.0035 | 3.663 | NO ACF fits | Very low z (0.043) + high intervening (143 pc/cm³); negative host; local cloud? |

---

### **SUMMARY - SAMPLE SUITABILITY FOR TWO-SCREEN JOINT MODEL:**

- **SUITABLE** (both scattering + scintillation measured, z known): **0/12** ❌
- **MARGINAL** (scattering only, z known): **3/12** (Chromatica, Hamilton, Oran)
- **UNSUITABLE** (no scintillation ACF fits): **6/12** (Isha, JohnDoeII, Mahi, Phineas, Whitney, Zach)
- **UNSUITABLE** (quality flags/severe degeneracies): **3/12** (Casey, Freya, Wilhelm)

### **CRITICAL CONCLUSIONS:**

1. **Scintillation bandwidth measured for only 3/12 bursts** (Casey, Freya, Wilhelm) — 9/12 lack independent α constraint
2. **All 3 bursts with scintillation show anomalies**: Casey FAIL χ²=69.2; Freya inverse ν (α_scint=−0.49, FAIL χ²=3.9, 52× legacy discrepancy); Wilhelm inverse ν (α_scint=−1.61, FAIL χ²=1.4, 10× legacy discrepancy)
3. **Refractive scattering dominates 2/3 measured bursts** (Freya, Wilhelm) — thin-screen diffractive model **INVALID**
4. **3 bursts have placeholder z=1.0** (Freya, Mahi, JohnDoeII) — cosmic/host DM unavailable; budgets unclosed
5. **DM budget failures**: 5+ bursts show over-constraints or negative host DM → sightline geometry invalid (mergers, outflows, core-extrapolation failures)

**Recommendation**: Your two-screen scattering+scintillation joint model cannot be applied to this sample. At most 3 bursts (Chromatica, Hamilton, Oran) have usable scattering fits, but NONE have both τ/α constraints AND validated scintillation Δν data. The 3 with measured scintillation all fail quality checks or show unphysical inverse scaling.

```
1	# CONTEXT.md Format
2	
3	## Structure
4	
5	```md
6	# {Context Name}
7	
8	{One or two sentence description of what this context is and why it exists.}
9	
10	## Language
11	
12	**Order**:
13	{A concise description of the term}
14	_Avoid_: Purchase, transaction
15	
16	**Invoice**:
17	A request for payment sent to a customer after delivery.
18	_Avoid_: Bill, payment request
19	
20	**Customer**:
21	A person or organization that places orders.
22	_Avoid_: Client, buyer, account
23	
24	## Relationships
25	
26	- An **Order** produces one or more **Invoices**
27	- An **Invoice** belongs to exactly one **Customer**
28	
29	## Example dialogue
30	
31	> **Dev:** "When a **Customer** places an **Order**, do we create the **Invoice** immediately?"
32	> **Domain expert:** "No — an **Invoice** is only generated once a **Fulfillment** is confirmed."
33	
34	## Flagged ambiguities
35	
36	- "account" was used to mean both **Customer** and **User** — resolved: these are distinct concepts.
37	```
38	
39	## Rules
40	
41	- **Be opinionated.** When multiple words exist for the same concept, pick the best one and list the others as aliases to avoid.
42	- **Flag conflicts explicitly.** If a term is used ambiguously, call it out in "Flagged ambiguities" with a clear resolution.
43	- **Keep definitions tight.** One sentence max. Define what it IS, not what it does.
44	- **Show relationships.** Use bold term names and express cardinality where obvious.
45	- **Only include terms specific to this project's context.** General programming concepts (timeouts, error types, utility patterns) don't belong even if the project uses them extensively. Before adding a term, ask: is this a concept unique to this context, or a general programming concept? Only the former belongs.
46	- **Group terms under subheadings** when natural clusters emerge. If all terms belong to a single cohesive area, a flat list is fine.
47	- **Write an example dialogue.** A conversation between a dev and a domain expert that demonstrates how the terms interact naturally and clarifies boundaries between related concepts.
48	
49	## Single vs multi-context repos
50	
51	**Single context (most repos):** One `CONTEXT.md` at the repo root.
52	
53	**Multiple contexts:** A `CONTEXT-MAP.md` at the repo root lists the contexts, where they live, and how they relate to each other:
54	
55	```md
56	# Context Map
57	
58	## Contexts
59	
60	- [Ordering](./src/ordering/CONTEXT.md) — receives and tracks customer orders
61	- [Billing](./src/billing/CONTEXT.md) — generates invoices and processes payments
62	- [Fulfillment](./src/fulfillment/CONTEXT.md) — manages warehouse picking and shipping
63	
64	## Relationships
65	
66	- **Ordering → Fulfillment**: Ordering emits `OrderPlaced` events; Fulfillment consumes them to start picking
67	- **Fulfillment → Billing**: Fulfillment emits `ShipmentDispatched` events; Billing consumes them to generate invoices
68	- **Ordering ↔ Billing**: Shared types for `CustomerId` and `Money`
69	```
70	
71	The skill infers which structure applies:
72	
73	- If `CONTEXT-MAP.md` exists, read it to find contexts
74	- If only a root `CONTEXT.md` exists, single context
75	- If neither exists, create a root `CONTEXT.md` lazily when the first term is resolved
76	
77	When multiple contexts exist, infer which one the current topic relates to. If unclear, ask.
78	
```

```
1	# ADR Format
2	
3	ADRs live in `docs/adr/` and use sequential numbering: `0001-slug.md`, `0002-slug.md`, etc.
4	
5	Create the `docs/adr/` directory lazily — only when the first ADR is needed.
6	
7	## Template
8	
9	```md
10	# {Short title of the decision}
11	
12	{1-3 sentences: what's the context, what did we decide, and why.}
13	```
14	
15	That's it. An ADR can be a single paragraph. The value is in recording *that* a decision was made and *why* — not in filling out sections.
16	
17	## Optional sections
18	
19	Only include these when they add genuine value. Most ADRs won't need them.
20	
21	- **Status** frontmatter (`proposed | accepted | deprecated | superseded by ADR-NNNN`) — useful when decisions are revisited
22	- **Considered Options** — only when the rejected alternatives are worth remembering
23	- **Consequences** — only when non-obvious downstream effects need to be called out
24	
25	## Numbering
26	
27	Scan `docs/adr/` for the highest existing number and increment by one.
28	
29	## When to offer an ADR
30	
31	All three of these must be true:
32	
33	1. **Hard to reverse** — the cost of changing your mind later is meaningful
34	2. **Surprising without context** — a future reader will look at the code and wonder "why on earth did they do it this way?"
35	3. **The result of a real trade-off** — there were genuine alternatives and you picked one for specific reasons
36	
37	If a decision is easy to reverse, skip it — you'll just reverse it. If it's not surprising, nobody will wonder why. If there was no real alternative, there's nothing to record beyond "we did the obvious thing."
38	
39	### What qualifies
40	
41	- **Architectural shape.** "We're using a monorepo." "The write model is event-sourced, the read model is projected into Postgres."
42	- **Integration patterns between contexts.** "Ordering and Billing communicate via domain events, not synchronous HTTP."
43	- **Technology choices that carry lock-in.** Database, message bus, auth provider, deployment target. Not every library — just the ones that would take a quarter to swap out.
44	- **Boundary and scope decisions.** "Customer data is owned by the Customer context; other contexts reference it by ID only." The explicit no-s are as valuable as the yes-s.
45	- **Deliberate deviations from the obvious path.** "We're using manual SQL instead of an ORM because X." Anything where a reasonable reader would assume the opposite. These stop the next engineer from "fixing" something that was deliberate.
46	- **Constraints not visible in the code.** "We can't use AWS because of compliance requirements." "Response times must be under 200ms because of the partner API contract."
47	- **Rejected alternatives when the rejection is non-obvious.** If you considered GraphQL and picked REST for subtle reasons, record it — otherwise someone will suggest GraphQL again in six months.
48

- **Inverse Δν scaling** (Freya Δν_CHIME 12.9 > Δν_DSA 7.0 MHz; Wilhelm 9.95 > 2.72) — Δν should *rise* with frequency (∝ν⁴). Inverse is either **real multi-screen/refractive physics** (→ the paper) or **one band's "Δν" isn't diffractive scintillation** (→ artifact). This is the crux.
- **Negative host DM** (Phineas, Whitney, Wilhelm, Zach) = `galaxies/v2_0` over-predicting intervening/cosmic → a DM-budget *model* issue, not necessarily data.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/jakobfaber/Developer/repos/github.com/jakobtfaber/dsa110-FLITS/CONTEXT.md",
  "content": "# CHIME/DSA Co-detection Scattering Context\n\nThe science domain for FLITS as applied to the CHIME–DSA-110 co-detected FRB sample: measuring pulse-broadening (scattering) and scintillation in the *same* burst at two widely separated frequency bands, to localize the turbulent screen(s) along the line of sight and tie them to the host/CGM/intervening environment.\n\n## Language\n\n**Co-detected FRB**:\nA fast radio burst seen by both CHIME (~0.6 GHz) and DSA-110 (~1.4 GHz). The sample is 12 such bursts (nicknames: casey, chromatica, freya, hamilton, isha, johndoeii, mahi, oran, phineas, whitney, wilhelm, zach).\n_Avoid_: \"joint burst\", \"dual detection\"\n\n**Two-band lever arm**:\nThe pair of well-separated observing frequencies (CHIME ~0.6 GHz, DSA ~1.4 GHz) for the same burst. The defining asset of this sample — it turns single-band degeneracies into measurable frequency scalings.\n\n**Scattering time** (`tau_1ghz`, τ):\nPulse-broadening timescale referenced to 1 GHz, in ms. Fitted by the scattering kernel (`burstfit.py`, models M0–M3).\n\n**Scattering index** (α):\nExponent in τ ∝ ν^(−α). Kolmogorov thin screen ≈ 4.0–4.4. Prior bounds 1.5–6.0; a fit railed at 6.0 means α is **unconstrained** (weak/unresolved scattering), not measured.\n_Avoid_: bare \"alpha\" — see Flagged ambiguities.\n\n**Scintillation bandwidth** (`dν`, Δν):\nDecorrelation bandwidth of the diffractive scintillation pattern, from the ACF. Should *increase* with frequency (≈ ∝ ν⁴ for a thin screen). Measured per band.\n\n**Modulation index** (m, m²):\nFractional intensity modulation of the scintillation; constrains how resolved the source is by the screen and bounds emission-region size (Nimmo et al. 2025 framework).\n\n**Consistency relation**:\nThe thin-screen identity 2π·τ·Δν ≈ C₁ (C₁ ≈ 1, order-unity). Used here as a **screen counter**: satisfied at a band ⇒ one screen plausibly does both broadening and scintillation; violated ⇒ ≥2 screens / different origins.\n\n**Screen**:\nA turbulent scattering layer. Classified by location: **Galactic** (Milky Way ISM, predicted by NE2001/NE2025), **host-CGM** (the FRB host galaxy's circumgalactic/ISM medium), or **intervening** (a foreground galaxy crossing the sightline).\n\n**Localization** (of a screen):\nAssigning a screen to Galactic vs host-CGM vs intervening, from the frequency scaling of τ and Δν, the consistency relation, the NE2001 Galactic floor, and the DM budget — *not* sky-position localization of the FRB.\n\n**Constraint ladder**:\nThe per-burst policy: always attempt the cheapest handle (consistency + ν-scaling); add effective-distance D_eff where a scintillation timescale is measurable; attempt a forward two-screen fit only where data quality supports it. Each burst is constrained as far as its data allows.\n\n**DM budget decomposition**:\nPartition of observed DM into MW ISM + MW halo + cosmic/IGM mean + intervening + host (residual). Produced by `galaxies/v2_0`.\n\n**Quality flag**:\nPer-fit PASS / MARGINAL / FAIL from the FLITS 3-level validation contract. FAIL fits are withheld from science claims.\n\n## Relationships\n\n- A **Co-detected FRB** is observed across the **two-band lever arm**, yielding a **scattering time** and **scintillation bandwidth** at each band.\n- The **consistency relation** applied per band counts **screens**; the frequency scaling of τ and Δν **localizes** each screen.\n- **Localization** is cross-checked against the **DM budget decomposition** and the NE2001/NE2025 Galactic floor.\n- The **constraint ladder** decides which handles each burst can support, gated by its **quality flags**.\n\n## Example dialogue\n\n> **Dev:** \"Freya's Δν is 12.9 MHz at CHIME but 7.0 MHz at DSA — that's inverse, Δν should rise with frequency. Is the two-screen model just wrong here?\"\n> **Domain expert:** \"Inverse scaling isn't a failure — it's a signal. Either the CHIME and DSA ACFs are sampling different screens, or the CHIME 'Δν' isn't diffractive scintillation at all. Don't call it a measurement until you've ruled out self-noise and RFI structure in that ACF.\"\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- **\"α\" / \"alpha\" is overloaded** — three distinct quantities: (1) **scattering index**, τ ∝ ν^(−α), ~4 Kolmogorov; (2) **scintillation-bandwidth scaling index**, Δν ∝ ν^(+α), ~4; (3) **intrinsic spectral index** of the burst flux. Always qualify which. Resolved convention: unqualified \"α\" = scattering index.\n- **\"Scintillation measured\"** — only 3/12 bursts have ACF-derived Δν (casey, freya, wilhelm). The other 9 are **not-yet-attempted**, NOT \"unsuitable\". Do not conflate.\n- **Freya τ**: legacy value 3.515 ms vs 2026-06 refit ~0.05–0.12 ms (≈30–50×). The refit supersedes; legacy values in older configs are stale.\n"
}

## [arxiv:2004.02862] Detection of Repeating FRB 180916.J0158+65 Down to Frequencies of 300 MHz
We report on the detection of seven bursts from the periodically active, repeating fast radio burst (FRB) source FRB 180916.J0158+65 in the 300-400-MHz frequency range with the Green Bank Telescope (GBT). Emission in multiple bursts is visible down to the bottom of the GBT band, suggesting that the cutoff frequency (if it exists) for FRB emission is lower than 300 MHz. Observations were conducted during predicted periods of activity of the source, and had simultaneous coverage with the Low Frequency Array (LOFAR) and the FRB backend on the Canadian Hydrogen Intensity Mapping Experiment (CHIME)

## [arxiv:2101.04082] Constraining the Fast Radio Burst (FRB) properties using the joint distributions of dispersion measure and fluence of the events detected at Parkes, ASKAP, CHIME and UTMOST
The Parkes, ASKAP, CHIME and UTMOST telescopes, which have all detected FRBs, each works at a different frequency and has a different detection criteria. Using simulations, we have combined the constraints from all four telescopes to identify an allowed range of model parameters (α, 33) for the FRB source population. Here α is the spectral index and 33 is the mean FRB energy in units of 10³³ J across a 2128 - 2848 MHz band in the FRB rest frame. We have considered several different FRB energy distributions, and also different scenarios for the scattering pulse broadening, the event rate densit

## [arxiv:2405.14182] A Heavily Scattered Fast Radio Burst Is Viewed Through Multiple Galaxy Halos
We present a multi-wavelength study of the apparently non-repeating, heavily scattered fast radio burst, FRB 20221219A, detected by the Deep Synoptic Array 110 (DSA-110). The burst exhibits a moderate dispersion measure (DM) of 706.7+0.6-0.6 pc cm⁻³ and an unusually high scattering timescale of τobs = 19.2-2.7+2.7 ms at 1.4 GHz. We associate the FRB with a Milky Way-like host galaxy at zhost = 0.554 of stellar mass log₁₀(M⋆, host) = 10.20+0.04-0.03 M⊙. Two bursts were detected from FRB 20220912A, one each on 2022 October 18 and 2022 October 25. The best-fit position is (R.A. J2000, decl. J2000) = (23:09:04.9, +48:42:25.4), with a 90% confidence error ellipse of ±2 arcsec and ±1 arcsec in right ascension and declination respectively. ## [arxiv:1903.12404] Constraining the Fast Radio Burst (FRB) properties using the detections at Parkes, ASKAP and CHIME
We demonstrate how the FRB redshift and fluence distributions from the Parkes, ASKAP and CHIME telescopes can be used together to constrain the properties of the FRB population. In this research, we build upon the bias correction method using pulse injections for the first CHIME/FRB catalog, to include correlations between properties, and to analyze the FRB population spectrum. This model includes six FRB properties: dispersion measure (DM), pulse width, scattering timescale, spectral index, spectral running, and fluence. By applying the multidimensional

## [arxiv:2107.10858] Modeling Fast Radio Burst Dispersion and Scattering Properties in the First CHIME/FRB Catalog
We present a Monte Carlo-based population synthesis study of fast radio burst (FRB) dispersion and scattering focusing on the first catalog of sources detected with the Canadian Hydrogen Intensity Mapping Experiment Fast Radio Burst (CHIME/FRB) project. We simulate intrinsic properties and propagation effects for a variety of FRB population models and compare the simulated distributions of dispersion measures (DMs) and scattering timescales with the corresponding distributions from the CHIME/FRB catalog. The catalog represents the first large sample, including bursts from repeaters and non-repeaters, observed in a single survey with uniform selection effects. ## [arxiv:2302.08386] Revealing the Dynamic Magneto-ionic Environments of Repeating Fast Radio Burst Sources through Multi-year Polarimetric Monitoring with CHIME/FRB

## [arxiv:2505.13297] The CHIME/FRB Discovery of the Extremely Active Fast Radio Burst Source FRB 20240114A

While multi-frequency characterizations of individual repeating FRBs like FRB 20201124A and FRB 20190520B exist, they typically treat frequency dependence within single or narrow bands rather than leveraging the wide, multi-instrument lever arm to solve for the screen distance ($$d_l$$).[1][2][3]\n\n**The Gap:** A 12-burst co-detected sample would be highly novel. > AGENT

Recon back. The honest landscape — and it reshapes the novelty claim:

**The core idea is NOT wide-open.** The direct precedent: **Tuntsov/"Two-Screen Scattering in CRAFT FRBs" (arXiv 2305.11477)** — a *population* two-screen study (n=10 ASKAP/CRAFT FRBs) applying the Masui 2015 + Ocker 2022 model to constrain screen distances. Plus the DM-budget-vs-scattering decomposition lineage is mature: **Cordes & Ocker 2021 (2108.01172, 2101.04784)** — halos/IGM don't scatter much, scattering is host-dominated. And the project's own simulation is built on **Pradeep 2025 (2505.04576)** Resolution-Power framework.

**What's genuinely novel — and it's narrower than "population two-screen":**
- CRAFT/ASKAP measures scattering at *one* band (1.4 GHz) and uses *Galactic scintillation* as the second screen — and **must assume α=4**.
- CHIME/DSA co-detection gives the **same burst at two widely separated bands (~0.6 & ~1.4 GHz)** → directly measures the **frequency scaling of both τ and Δν**, empirically pinning α per burst instead of assuming it. *That two-band-same-burst leverage is the real contribution.*
- **No published CHIME/DSA co-detection scattering/scintillation study exists** (confirmed). First-sample status is real.
- The **inverse-Δν / refractive anomalies have a theoretical home** — CGM cloudlet refractive lensing (Sammons/Cordes 2309.07256) + Pradeep RP — so they're framable as physics, not just noise.

**Caveat (data reality bites the novelty):** the clean "two-band empirical α" pitch is partly aspirational — α rails for ~half the sample and only 3/12 have Δν so far. The validation campaign decides how much of the methodological novelty actually lands.

Must-cite for positioning: Masui+2015, Ocker+2022b, CRAFT 2305.11477, Pradeep+2025, Cordes&Ocker (2108.01172 / 2101.04784), Nimmo+2024 (Nature, 2406.11053), DSA multi-halo 2405.14182.

Capstone fork — how to position the paper against that landscape:

  "content": "# Position the co-detection paper on two-band leverage, not population two-screen\n\n**Status:** accepted\n\nThe co-detected CHIME/DSA FRB scattering paper is positioned around the **empirical measurement of the scattering index α (and screen localization) from the same burst seen simultaneously at ~0.6 and ~1.4 GHz** — the one thing prior population two-screen work could not do.\n\n## Context\n\nPopulation two-screen scattering+scintillation localization already exists: \"Two-Screen Scattering in CRAFT FRBs\" (arXiv 2305.11477, n=10 ASKAP) applies the Masui (2015) + Ocker (2022b) model to constrain screen distances. The DM-budget-vs-scattering decomposition lineage (Cordes & Ocker 2108.01172, 2101.04784; halos/IGM contribute little scattering, host dominates) is mature. So \"we do population two-screen localization + DM budget\" is **not** a novel claim.\n\n## Decision\n\nLead with the **two-band-same-burst leverage**: CRAFT and the Ocker lineage measure scattering in one band and must **assume α≈4** (Galactic scintillation supplies the second screen). CHIME/DSA co-detection measures the **frequency scaling of both τ and Δν directly** across a ~2.3× frequency ratio, pinning α per burst empirically. Secondary framing: first CHIME/DSA co-detected sample (no such scattering/scintillation study published) with per-burst tiered localization + host/CGM context.\n\n## Consequences\n\n- The introduction must explicitly differentiate from CRAFT 2305.11477 and Cordes&Ocker — reviewers will raise them.\n- The strength of the headline scales with how many bursts deliver a non-railed α; the validation campaign (re-measure anomalous ACFs, run scintillation on the 9 un-measured bursts, finish Monte-Carlo recovery) gates this. If α rails for most of the sample, the paper degrades gracefully to a first-sample census, but that is the fallback, not the pitch.\n- The inverse-Δν / refractive anomalies are held for a possible second paper (CGM cloudlet lensing + Pradeep 2025 RP), not the lead claim — see [[constraint-ladder]] in CONTEXT.md.\n"
}

The sample (ground truth)\n\n12 co-detected FRBs (casey, chromatica, freya, hamilton, isha, johndoeii, mahi, oran, phineas, whitney, wilhelm, zach). CHIME ~0.6 GHz + DSA ~1.4 GHz on the same burst — the two-band lever arm is the defining asset. 234 GB data external (iacobus), none in-repo.\n\nStatus: 11/12 joint scattering fits; host search 9/12; DM budget 12/12; TOA cross-match 12/12 (ad-hoc, not via the stub). **Scintillation Δν measured for only 3/12** (casey, freya, wilhelm). 3 fits FAIL (casey, freya, wilhelm). 3 placeholder z=1.0 (freya, mahi, johndoeii).\n\nData-quality flags surfaced from the result files:\n- **α rails at 6.0** (upper bound) for chromatica, freya, hamilton, mahi → scattering weak/unresolved → these become **upper limits**.\n- **Inverse Δν scaling** (freya Δν_CHIME 12.9 > Δν_DSA 7.0 MHz; wilhelm 9.95 > 2.72) → either real multi-screen/refractive physics or one band's \"Δν\" is not diffractive — **must be adjudicated**.\n- **Negative host DM** (phineas, whitney, wilhelm, zach) → `galaxies/v2_0` over-predicting intervening/cosmic; a **model** issue, not necessarily data.\n- **Freya τ**: legacy 3.515 ms vs refit ~0.05–0.12 ms (≈30–50×); refit supersedes.\n\n## C. To-be-developed (software)\n\nOrdered by leverage-per-effort:\n\n1. **Wire the two-screen layer** (consistency relation + ν-scaling + modulation→size) into the pipeline. Funcs exist in `scintillation/scint_analysis/analysis.py`, just not called. *Low cost, unblocks the headline.*\n2. **NE2025 Galactic-floor integration** — wire `ne2025/query_ne2025_scint.py` so each burst's measured scattering is compared to the predicted MW floor (Galactic vs extragalactic split). *Low cost.*\n3. **Scintillation campaign tooling** — fix `flits/batch` scint config-gen stub (`batch_runner.py:88,197`) so the mature scint pipeline runs over all 12.\n4. **ACF anomaly re-validation harness** — RFI + self-noise + off-pulse checks for the 3 measured Δν, to certify diffractive vs artifact. *New, small.*\n5. **Finish Monte-Carlo parameter-recovery** (`simulation/monte_carlo.py` aggregation) → defensible τ/Δν error bars AND upper limits.\n6. **DM-budget model fix** — diagnose negative-host-DM sightlines in `galaxies/v2_0` (intervening/cosmic over-prediction).\n7. Debt (not science): `flits/` wrapper consolidation; τ(ν) batch placeholder (`analysis_logic.py:110`); `crossmatching/` left as stub unless geometric-delay localization becomes a goal.\n\n## D. To-be-explored (science)\n\n| Direction | Handle | Prior art / novelty |\n|---|---|---|\n| **Empirical α per burst** from two-band τ scaling | scattering joint fit | **Novel angle** — CRAFT/Ocker assume α=4; we measure it (ADR-0001) |\n| **Screen localization** (Galactic/host/intervening) | consistency + ν-scaling + NE2025 floor | Method exists (Masui 2015, Ocker 2022b, CRAFT 2305.11477); two-band application is the new part |\n| **Population screen census** across 12 | tiered constraint ladder | DM-budget lineage mature (Cordes & Ocker 2108.01172) — differentiator is co-detection + two-band |\n| **Inverse-Δν / refractive anomalies** | multi-screen / RP | CGM cloudlet lensing (Sammons 2309.07256), Pradeep 2025 RP — **held for paper 2** |\n| **Emission-region size** from modulation index | Nimmo 2025 framework | Nimmo+2024 (Nature 2406.11053) — bonus for brightest bursts |\n| **Intervening galaxy / MgII → excess scattering** | `mgii_inventory.csv` + budget | DSA multi-halo precedent (2405.14182) |\n\n## E. Phased plan\n\n**Phase 0 — Reconciliation (done here):** inventory, capability matrix, prior-art recon, positioning (ADR-0001).\n\n**Phase 1 — Targeted validation (gate before science):**\n- Re-measure the 3 anomalous ACFs (casey/freya/wilhelm) with RFI/self-noise/off-pulse checks → certify Δν diffractive or flag artifact.\n- Run scintillation pipeline over the other 9 bursts (needs B.3).\n- Finish Monte-Carlo recovery (B.5) → error bars + upper-limit framework.\n- Wire NE2025 floor + consistency relation (B.1, B.2).\n- **Exit gate:** every burst has τ (value or upper limit) + Δν (value or upper limit) + PASS/MARGINAL/FAIL, all through the runtime validation contract and figure-review gate.\n\n**Phase 2 — Tiering + localization:**\n- Assign GOLD (clean τ+Δν+z) vs SILVER per the matrix.\n- Per-burst constraint ladder: consistency screen-count; ν-scaling Galactic-vs-extragalactic; D_eff and forward-fit where data bears it.\n- Cross-check localization vs DM budget (B.6 first).\n\n**Phase 3 — Population synthesis + write-up:**\n- Empirical α distribution; screen-location census; host/CGM attribution.\n- Differentiate explicitly from CRAFT 2305.11477 + Cordes & Ocker.\n- Anomalies documented, deferred to paper 2 unless validation promotes them.\n\n**Execution note:** Phases 1–2 are burst-parallel over 12 bursts → use a dynamic workflow paired with `.claude/workflows/fit-verify.js` (adversarial re-check of every `*_fit_results.json`), with a `/goal` completion condition = all 12 bursts at a defined constraint level, not a partial set.\n"

```
1	#!/usr/bin/env python3
2	"""
3	query_ne2025_scint.py  –  v3, NE2025, sexagesimal-aware
4	-------------------------------------------------------
5	Predict the Milky-Way scattering floor (tau_scatt and scintillation bandwidth
6	Dnu_scint) for a list of sky positions using NE2025
7	[Ocker & Cordes, ADS:2026ApJ..1002....3O] via mwprop.nemod.NE2025.
8	
9	For an extragalactic source (FRB) the relevant quantity is the TOTAL Galactic
10	scattering integrated to the edge of the disk, so we call NE2025 in d->DM mode
11	(ndir<0) with a large distance (30 kpc, capped at the Galactic boundary). The MW
12	floor is independent of the source DM, so no 'dm' column is needed.
13	
14	INPUT  : CSV/TSV with either …
15	         • a single column  'coord'  containing ICRS strings
16	           e.g.  01h24m50.45s +72d39m14.1s
17	         • OR columns  ra, dec   (sexagesimal or degrees)
18	         • OR legacy  ra_deg, dec_deg  (degrees)
19	
20	OUTPUT : CSV with tau_ms_<freq>MHz and bw_kHz_<freq>MHz (the predicted MW floor).
21	"""
22	
23	import argparse
24	import re
25	
26	import astropy.units as u
27	import pandas as pd
28	from astropy.coordinates import SkyCoord
29	from mwprop.nemod.NE2025 import ne2025
30	
31	EDGE_KPC = 30.0  # integrate to the Galactic boundary (NE2025 caps at the edge)
32	
33	
34	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
35	def coord_from_row(row):
36	    """
37	    Return an astropy SkyCoord from a dataframe row containing any of:
38	      - 'coord'  (full ICRS string)
39	      - 'ra', 'dec'           (sexagesimal or degrees)
40	      - 'ra_str', 'dec_str'
41	      - 'ra_deg', 'dec_deg'
42	    """
43	    # Case 1: single 'coord' column
44	    if "coord" in row and pd.notna(row["coord"]):
45	        return SkyCoord(row["coord"], frame="icrs")
46	
47	    # Helper: find any matching RA/Dec pair
48	    variants = [("ra", "dec"), ("ra_str", "dec_str"), ("ra_deg", "dec_deg")]
49	    for ra_key, dec_key in variants:
50	        if ra_key in row and dec_key in row and pd.notna(row[ra_key]):
51	            ra_val, dec_val = row[ra_key], row[dec_key]
52	            # Detect degrees vs sexagesimal by simple regex (contains h or : ?)
53	            sexa = bool(re.search("[hms:]", str(ra_val)))
54	            unit = (u.deg, u.deg) if not sexa else None
55	            return SkyCoord(ra_val, dec_val, unit=unit, frame="icrs")
56	
57	    raise ValueError("Row is missing a recognisable coordinate column set.")
58	
59	
60	def query_single(coord_icrs, freq_mhz, alpha=4.4):
61	    """Return (tau_scatt_ms, Dnu_scint_kHz) of the MW floor at freq_mhz.
62	
63	    Uses NE2025's native TAU and SBW (both reported @1 GHz) and scales them by
64	    nu^-alpha / nu^+alpha (alpha=4.4 = 22/5; scattering_functions2020.py:90,
65	    tauiss ~ nu^-4.4). Taking SBW from the model preserves NE2025's C1=1.16,
66	    unlike re-deriving Dnu_d = 1/(2*pi*tau) which implicitly assumes C1=1.
67	    """
68	    gl, gb = coord_icrs.galactic.l.value, coord_icrs.galactic.b.value
69	    Dk, Dv, Du, Dd = ne2025(ldeg=gl, bdeg=gb, dmd=EDGE_KPC, ndir=-1, classic=False, dmd_only=False)
70	    nu_ghz = freq_mhz / 1000.0
71	    tau_ms = Dv["TAU"] * nu_ghz ** (-alpha)  # ms
72	    bw_kHz = Dv["SBW"] * nu_ghz**alpha * 1e3  # MHz @1GHz -> kHz at freq
73	    return tau_ms, bw_kHz
74	
75	
76	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
77	def main():
78	    p = argparse.ArgumentParser()
79	    p.add_argument("infile", help="Input CSV/TSV with sky positions")
80	    p.add_argument("--freq", type=float, default=600.0, help="Frequency in MHz (default 600)")
81	    p.add_argument("--alpha", type=float, default=4.4, help="Scattering index α (default 4.4)")
82	    p.add_argument("--out", default="ne2025_results.csv", help="Output CSV file name")
83	    args = p.parse_args()
84	
85	    # Load table (auto-detect separator)
86	    df = pd.read_csv(args.infile, sep=None, engine="python")
87	
88	    taus, bws = [], []
89	    for _idx, row in df.iterrows():
90	        coord = coord_from_row(row)
91	        tau_ms, bw_kHz = query_single(coord, args.freq, args.alpha)
92	        taus.append(tau_ms)
93	        bws.append(bw_kHz)
94	
95	    df[f"tau_ms_{args.freq:.0f}MHz"] = taus
96	    df[f"bw_kHz_{args.freq:.0f}MHz"] = bws
97	    df.to_csv(args.out, index=False)
98	    print(f"[ok] {len(df)} positions processed -> '{args.out}'")
99	
100	
101	if __name__ == "__main__":
102	    main()
103	
```
